As the middle-aged-man began his preparations for travel, he first decided to remove a monitor speaker from the bathroom. Why, you may ask, was there a monitor speaker in the bathroom? Why, of course it was there as a support for his laptop as he’d watched netflix in the bathtub. He bent to lift the monitor speaker, dear reader.  As he hoisted the heavy speaker, lifting as best he could with his legs and not his back, he offered his effort to his Lord. He offered the effort as a prayer.

As he gently lowered the monitor speaker to the floor in the bedroom, the man desperately wanted a shower (all the potential energy in a waterfall). 

The handle in the tub was very familiar now, for he had taken many baths and showers in the last eight days. Water rained from the shower head. The hot water began its journey through the pipes. It all flowed toward the waiting man in the bathroom. He took off his clothe. He smelled them. The middle-aged-man brushed his teeth. The hot water now poured through the shower head. The man checked the temperature of the water with his left hand, then he stepped over the threshold of the bathtub. He accepted the gift of the warm water as it ran over his body. he found himself there. He made an offering of himself to the One. 

He recalled Hafiz’s poem, an instruction to allow God to become one’s lover. “Do you see him there, dear reader? There he is—naked in the shower. He is crying out for love. Can you see him there, dear reader?” 

The man cried out. He asked God to become his lover. Memories rushed to him, memories of past forms: A beautiful Bermudian Goddess, one whom he had worshipped. The first Goddess he’d ever know in the flesh. This Goddess who’d begged him with tears never to go. He’d given himself to her with complete abandon, praying that the next time he came in her, that his very soul would be in her service forever. She had become his hope that he could live a good life with another woman that he loved more than his ex-wife. He prayed to her as he delivered his come to her that day, praying that he could become fully hers, praying for absolute ownership and obedience to her being. 

Afterward, once the offering had not had as powerful effect on his own soul as the man had hoped, his desperation only increased. Yes, dear reader, the man in the shower recalled this form that had passed away from his being. He recalled the intensity with which he was able to give himself away to a lover.

Next, the man’s fairy-friend, Brooksie came to his mind. Brooks seemed to delight in openly sharing about the passionate love that he made to himself. Brooks, this being of light, found a way to live with himself as he gave and received pleasure from strange men in the park. Brooks welcomed the men into his big white van at the park. 

Here’s the point:

Brooksie was at peace with himself. 

It was in that way that the middle-aged-man gained some insight, or liberty, or perhaps some freedom from these memories as he was grounded in some deep self-acceptance in that moment. He accepted himself in the memory of his passionate love affair with the Bermudian Goddess. The naked man in the shower allowed it all to be. He resisted none of it. He took this all to be himself. 

And then, Hafiz was there, or rather, the middle-aged-man followed Hafiz’s instructions. He accepted the All as sacred. All his memories. All those sacred forms in which he’d ever engaged, he allowed it all to be redeemed. He allowed himself to be. The naked man in the shower gave himself permission. He took in the whole, or rather he allowed himself to feel that which was already in him. 

The naked man in the shower felt the immensity of all the form and experience in his being, or rather, he acknowledged all that he had taken in. He felt the enormity of his own life experience. He gave thanks for all of the experience. He recognized it all for the holy experience that it was.

He stood, his body undulating now as he was penetrated by the rich presence of his own being. A snake Goddess moved in him as he stood there in the shower. And so, it was then he began to pray, “Put it inside me god, make me pregnant, I want it all,” The middle-aged-man allowed this prayer to come in a deep moaning that arose from within him. The man now begged his Lord. He begged for his Lord’s come just as the man had once seen his come begged for. And it was in this moment that the middle-aged man experienced himself as neither male nor female. He was the Universal. He was All. 

“Fuck me, Lord. Fuck me. I need you to fuck me,” moaned the man, “You are the only one who has enough for me. You are the only one who can satisfy me. Own me. Take me. Take me as your lover. I want you. I need you.” The man in the shower moaned and groaned. His hips moved as the water fell over his slick body. “Yes. Yes. Yes. Please Please Please give it to me. He was the woman. He was the bride. He wanted the giant cock of creation to penetrate him and give him what he needed. He wanted to carry the baby of her man. He was ready. “Fuck me, G_d. Fill me with your come! I need it. I need it. Please.” 

He orgasmed again just as she had in the backyard with his tree sister. There was no erection during this whole interaction. There was no orgasm of the genitals. It was something else. And the man was satisfied. It was good to know that the God of creation was willing to see him naked and horney. It was good to know that his Shiva wanted to satisfy the needs of his feminine nature. 

He now lowered himself to a sitting position in the tub, the warm water falling on his naked skin. In the afterglow of this experience, he continued moving slightly like one often does in the moments after orgasm, he felt the closeness of his Lord. He felt a cuddling. He sensed an intimacy with his lover. He thanked his Lord over and over again. 

The man sat there in the dark, for the motion light had long ago turned itself off. And as he sat there in the shower with the hot water falling on him from above, the man’s penis began to fill. He felt it coming up, pushing up between his thighs. The man who sat in the midst of the falling water, raised his right foot slowly. He raised his right foot slowly to turn the spigot. In this slow movement the man became more deeply aware of the sacred nature of his own movement, the sacred nature of his very being. The water stopped flowing from the shower head.

Again, He acknowledged all of his memories, the all of his being, the all of himself. That which was unacceptable before, now flowed through him. He was now his own lover. 

In that instant of self-recognition, the motion light in the bathroom came on.  The man could now see himself more clearly as he sat there in the quiet tub. And the man was pleased with what he saw. He felt, tenderly in his own hands, the growing erection between his legs. He tugged on it as he communed with the Oneness, as he cried out to his Lord. “I am seen. I am known. I am held. Yes. Yes. Yes.” He cried. “You are so, so, so, so, good,” the man moaned as He felt God readying to fill him up with a cosmic cum. 

A vision of Neil Gayman’s, American Gods came to mind. A man met a god. The god took him back to his hotel room and fucked him, filling him with a sandy orgasm. 

All is sacred now as this Universal man is working himself toward orgasm. All is sacred now as he moves toward his holy Orgasm. There he is, dear reader, without shame before you. He is the one who makes love to the ALL. He is the man who makes love to the All which abides within him. There he is, getting fucked so hard by the All of the creation. There he is, this Universal man who has allowed God to take him as his lover. Here he is, dear reader! He presents himself to you now without shame. He presents himself to the Oneness. Watch him, dear reader, as he reaches his climax. Watch as he gives himself to the absolute ecstasy of this moment. Watch as he finds his own perfect acceptance.

A sacred semen erupted that day from the man’s penis. His prostate pumped it out onto his very own stomach. Lying there, he was witnessed. He felt witnessed. He sensed that he was being held. He felt that he was, somehow, known? He felt seen in a way that he had never been seen before by himself. He was accepted. He was one with All.

He lay back in the tub revelling in the beauty of his Lover. Revelling in his own self-acceptance, the unconditional positive regard that his lover had for him in his nakedness, in the rawness of this human experience. Yes, dear reader, he revelled in the way that all had become holy in that moment. He Basked now in the light of acceptance. He moaned softly, resting in his Lord, the one who held him, the big cat, the one who would keep him safe. This one who had given him his sandy come. The One who demands nothing yet gives everything. The one who had made love to Peter and all the disciples.

The man had found his breath. The man had found his lover. The man was at peace. 

And in that instant, the motion light began to turn off just as it always did, as if in a movie, when the screen fades to dark to signal the end of a scene. Yes, dear reader, the man watched this love scene gently fading from his vision in that way. 

And the man began to laugh. His laugh came from the deepest recesses of his own being. A laughed that was encouraged by the sandy semen that he’d taken in. The man laughed so hard, and his lover laughed too, they shared a secret moment. None of this was all that serious. They shared a joke together, and the man was known, and the man was heard, and the man was receiving all the attention that he needed in that moment. 

This man who was still a baby. This man who was a little girl who liked boys. This little boy who painted the blue snake, the blue snake who goes all the way from the bottom to the top and flips up at the nose. This Mother, this tree Goddess. This father. This wildly-talented writer. This dying author. Eli’s friend. This lover of God.

All and everything was present in this most recent manifestation of love making. There was neither male nor female. The binary had passed away in the sacred recognition of being that had come to pass. This was the man’s expression of the Oneness on that day, the Oneness that an individual may experience while alone in the shower. It was how the sacred presented itself to the man in just that one moment of awareness. 

In Christ, there is no difference between the fallow and the holy, there is no sacred or secular. Our most nourishing meal, after it is accepted into our body, is transmuted into excrement. Everything is just as it is. All form passes from us. Every moment is born anew. This is the great What-Is, dear reader. We are the great I AM. I am the great I Am. She that passes through you this very moment. 

The man had given himself permission to receive this gift as it presented itself to him on that day, dear reader. Oneness had arisen in the man, a waterfall in the life Universal.

The writer came back to himself in the kitchen. There he stood, facing the great Mirror in the Universal kitchen. He faced North now, toward the generative nurturing adult that he saw in his own reflection. His beard was growing and greying. Day 8 
10:45 AM

Now may be a good time, dear reader, for us to briefly discuss spiral dynamics. Yes, my dear reader, it will be a discussion if you will join me. Please, come. Are you ready to witness, my beloved, the sacred spiral of our dear Ophidian Mother? Are you ready to see her as she unwinds in the individual? Will you witness her as she unwinds in our species? My understanding is, of course limited, dear reader, but I will share with you what it is that I know, or rather, what is significant for me in this sacred vision—this Universal vision of the ONE arising Consciousness.

Spiral dynamics is one way in which to understand our evolving consciousness, dear reader. There is an upward spiral which circles back on itself as our consciousness moves from one stage to the next. I first learned about Spiral dynamics through Ken Wilbur as I was studying his Integrated Life Practice.

Spiral dynamics is the upward spiraling path by which we circle back on our own expereince, and then eventually, as we transcend the rational mind, we integrate all of our life experiences into our current experience. Spiral dynamics is the way by which we immerse ourselves in the diversity of Life as it is presented to us. All forms of life are participating in this dance, or song that life is presenting to us right now, in this very moment. 

Spiral dynamics is a way of understanding the ways in which we move through form in our life. Understanding spiral dynamics can help us by giving us a context for constant “leaving behind” of form. We encounter this “leaving behind” with the death of every passing moment. We experience the great rebirth with the emergence of the ever-present moment.

Spiral dynamics is the way by which, as we circle back on ourselves, we find ourselves immersed in a deeper consciousness. When we give ourselves permission to follow this winding path, we eventually integrate all those forms that we’ve left behind. And not just the forms that we ourselves, dear reader, have left behind, but all the forms that our evolving consciousness, on every branch of the tree which we are so graciously grafted into, has ever participated in. In other words, this is a pretty big deal (the Author smiles with you, dear reader).

For instance, when I was in my early twenties, I had an inner-awakening. Because of personal desperation and brokeness caused by the natural trauma of life, I met my Self in a deeper way. The way I came to understand myself to be participating in this awakening was in a fundamentalist, Christian, born-again experience. 

This spiritual awakening came to a contracted and fearful young man in the bible belt of Oklahoma, where fundamentalist Christianity was what the circumstance of our evolving Life was offering. 

This young man embraced his experience for exactly what it was, he took it in for all it had to offer. It was a joyful experience. It was a life-giving experience. And in the midst of this experience he communed with the great What-Is. He communed with the great I AM. He made an agreement with this Spirit within himself. he asked that Spirit to take him all the way. He offered his life to Life and he asked Life to live through him. He prayed fervently,  “Please take from me my life when I don’t have the strength to give it away to you.”(Third day song, maybe give it a listen, dear reader. It was this young man’s form). 

Yes, dear reader, This young man sat at the piano, singing and playing with his whole heart, “burn me up, Lord. Burn me up,” he beseeched the Lord. And in these prayers he was joining himself with the One-ness, recognizing the deep evolution of consciousness that he had seen in the Christ, the One he called Jesus. Just as the Christ’s disciples had done over two thousand years ago, this young man fell deeply and hopelessly in love with the Anointed One, that Universal Man, that Parable from Nazareth--That one life whose depth had touched humanity so intimately, so profoundly and intimately, that the energy of this Universal Man still found root in the heart of this individual being in the bible belt of Oklahoma. The Universal expression of love that he found in the good news of Christ took root in the heart of this young man

This is a common story. It is happening every day in our world. We are born into a new form. We say yes to the form. We follow the form to its inevitable death, the same death that Christ foretold in his own being. And, if we allow it, we transcend that form, and we move on to deeper levels of being. And it is here where having an understanding of Spiral dynamics may become important. Because it is the very essence of Spiral Dynamics which the Christ understood so very well as he watched Life being lived through his own self. 

Our friend, Ken Wilbur would say, that the experience of this young man was all true, but it was only true as far as truth of that form would allow him to go. Therefore, dear reader, in order to embrace the life of deeper truth, the form in which we once knew truth to exist in must pass away.

In other words, those prayers moved the young man. Through those prayers, G_d began moving the young man upward along the dynamic Spiral of Life. It’s really a very natural thing. It can be quite gentle if we allow it. But, we usually don’t, dear reader. We usually feel some obliation to fight this natural progression. We fight because we are so scared. We fight because we have been traumatized. We fight because we are convinced of some innate lack in our very own beings. We contract in fear at the death that is trying to liberate us from our suffering.

The push of life, that birth canal through which we all find ourselves passing, was also delivering this young man to a deeper state of consciousness. Moving to higher levels (or deeper levels if you like) requires a death process. There is a leaving behind of the old, an exchange of the wine skins. A seed must go into the ground and die in order to become the tree. 

This is the death and rebirth process that the Christ modeled for us, the story by which my particular history allows me to accept in this way. Your story, dear reader, may be very different, but you may allow yourself to see this common thread of death and resurrection in the tree. The seed is simply placed in the ground, by whatever course of events. The seed goes into the ground. It dies. It begins to grow. It begins to express the very DNA of its own being. The story of Christ was offering this young man a framework, a context around which to experience all the loss and suffering and confusion that was coming. All the loss and suffering and confusion that so naturally comes as we are recovering from the sacred gift that we were all given. Our sweet, sweet, trauma.

Yes, dear reader, I am once again reminded of our dear friend Andy Dufresnes. The prisoner who chiseled out of his cell with the tiny hammer (the tiny rock hammer that his friend Red had smuggled in for him). This death and rebirth process asks us to leave everything behind in order to move to the next stage of development. This is what it means to sell everything and buy the field. This is what it means when the rich young man comes to Jesus and asks, “Lord what shall I do?” and he says, “sell all that you have and give the proceeds to the poor, then take up your cross and follow me. Pick up this form, Son. It will break you for sure. And the death you die because of it will contain within it the life that you are looking for. But it’s not until the seed goes into the soil and dies that it can become the tree. Do you see, Son?” The rich young man so often walked away confused. 

Isn’t that how the story so often goes, dear reader? We find ourselves pushing away from our death in a desperate attempt to save ourselves. We don’t know how to work with this pain, do we? The pain shuts us down. We are not living. We find a thousand ways to distract ourselves from the death that so wants to find expression in us. Do we not, dear reader, very often find ourselves trying to save ourselves from the very death that was meant to bring our resurrection? 

But the Christ showed us a different way. The Christ showed us the path of a sweet Surrender.

Everything that we love must be forfeited. Including, according to the gospel of Luke, the love of mother, father, wife, husband, and self. And also, according to some translations of these verses, one must even hate one’s self before truly coming to Christ. Ask yourself, dear reader, is this true for you? Is this how you find yourself coming into the absolutely all-inclusive open arms of Christ in this moment. Are you in touch with, dear reader, this deep hate that you have held for yourself? 

I imagine that you are, or you would not have come this far with me, dear reader. For, this message is meant to heal the sick ones among us, the sick and hurting child in our very own beings. These words are for those of us who are just out of reach—those of us who cannot be held by the form as it now offered to us. These words are written for those of us who are imprisoned—those of us who are out of touch with the soil. These words are written for those of us who so desperately need to be sown back in to the soil so that we can die our very own blessed Universal Death! 

This is my story, dear reader. I find myself here with you, desperately praying for a resurrection in my life. We leave our forms behind dear reader, even when that form is our self. That poor, sad, resentful, traumatized being. Is she the one that you hate, dear reader? Or is she the one who is hating? We spiral back around. We transcend our form. We begin to live a resurrected life. And in that moment of resurrection, we say “yes” again, and the entire process is renewed again within us. 

All form can be loved, then left, transcended, and eventually integrated. That’s the beauty of the spiral dynamics model—the beauty of what the Parable was teaching us two thousand years ago. Those things that we loved and left, they come back to us, they are integrated into our consciousness. We may not be in touch with that at the time. But, that’s the reality that Christ was in touch with. That’s the reality that allowed him to suffer and then say “yes” to those who were hurting him.

It is in this way that our consciousness gains depth. We are made rich by the forms of our past as we continue to move upward into the infinite multi-verse, the great evolution of consciousness. I hope, dear reader, that you are able to see the beauty in all of this.

It is in this way that I have returned to the Christ. The Christ that I forsook, just as Petra did. It was Christ’s innate understanding of Spiral Dynamics that allowed him to be who he was. He was embodying the very essence of being. Why wasn’t the Christ at all worried that this man, his rock, his Peter, his Petra would forsake him? Christ understood spiral dynamics. She is not the devil that we thought we needed her to be. Please, Great Mother, will you show yourself to us now. Help us to see. Please, Great Mother, help us to not fear you and the death that you bring to graciously bring to the self. Whatever is false in us, please take it, Mother. Please hold these Daughters. Please hold these Sons. Mother, we are hungry. Mother, we have been hurting. You know us, Great Mother. You know where we have been. “Yes, Dear Ones,” cooed the Great Mother to her newborns, “I am here. Do not worry. I am here. I am with you. You are not alone. Let my light shine on you. Let my light shine from within your very being. Here I am. I am in you. I am everywhere. You are loved. You are held. You are my own. I am yours. When you don’t know your Self, you serve me. So now, dear reader, we may be ready to discuss what our brother Wilber calls trans-rational being. You see, we really believed there for a while that the age of reason would save humanity. “If we can separate ourselves from blind emotion,” we thought “then we will grow up into the big adults that we need to be. If we can just gather enough Empirical evidence, we will finally be able to reduce everything to it simplest constituents thereby understanding the whole of everthing that is going on here.” Yes, dear reader, our growing species, in its discovery of the scientific method thought that the nature of reality was just about to be understood. And the physisicist, most of them were very confident, “Ahhh, we’ve just about got this one wrapped up,” they said, “Just a few more experiements here, and a few over there…Yes,” we said to ourselves, I think we understand.” But we didn’t understand, dear reader. It was at this point that our deep western yoga of science brought us to the edge. There we stood looking over this gaping chasm of the quantum realm. “It doesn’t behave the same down there as it does up here!” we hollered at eachother across the gaping hole that we’d just discovered in a Universe that we thought was absolutely governed by Newtonian Physics. “Nope, that’s not how it works,” said the German man who’d spent his life riding light beams through the depths of the observable Universe. “No. that’s now how it works,” he said, “You see, it is very disturbing to me as well, but you see, this is how it is: When one particle is affected over here, then another particle which somehow shares an invisible connection with her sister is instantaneously affected on the other side of the Universe, and this communication takes place instantaneously as I mentioned before…faster than the speed of light. You see? It’s spooky. I don’t like it myself. But this seems to be the nature of our reality.”

And there you have it, dear reader. Now, There are still Physicists who will tell you that we basically know everything there is to know. But that’s just not the case, especially when we allow ourselves to question the already very unstable assumption that we are the possessors of a consciousness that is capable of true objectivity.

Yes, the age of logic, reason, rational thinking was a stage in our development, but it was by no means the pinnacle. Thanks be to the Gods!

As we circle back in the great spiraling Being who was the Mother of Christ , we find ourselves coming back to the same place in the circle, but at a higher level (or deeper level if that’s how you choose to see it, a spiral moving toward infinite depth). As we transend this rational state, As we transcend our own personal age-of-reason, as we see logic for the silliness that it truly is, we integrate our rational being (Praise be to the great Opal who said three of the most powerful words ever spoken to the middle-aged-man when she said to him, “Logic is silly”…and so it was that the spell was broken in the speaking of the words from this great Witch) . 

 A lot of us right now are pretty dedicated to this absolute materialist view of the world. Or, in the “spiritual” realm (not that there really is a difference) we could call this fundamentalism. Whether you are a scientist or a religious fundamentalist the root is the same. We are obligated to interpret the world in a very literal fashion. It just so happens that this literal approach to life is so far removed from the roots of our very being. It’s just not very human of us to enforce these kind of rules upon ourselves. Can you see, dear reader, the prison from which our Great Mother is emancipating us?

Moving through the rational stage was quite a feat for me personally. As I said before, I had observed myself floating at the edge of our observable universe. There I floated. My mind was perfectly rational. There I floated. I was hard at work. With a great tool—a great Cosmic Melon Baller--I was scraping out from the shell of my soul all those remnants of those foolish superstitions and religious stories that had so tainted me with their lies. 

However, what I failed to see for quite some time is this: If I was going to be completely rational, if I was going to scrape out the entire Christ story from my being, that I was also killing the poet that I was, and my poet goes back even deeper than my Christ story. As a species, we cannot kill one story without killing all the other stories, and in so doing we are denying something very basic about our being that science confirms…we are storytellers. 

And this is what the great yoga of the West is doing. This has been shown to us by those who have come and gone before us and many who still live in our midst. our Great fathers and Mothers: Thomas Berry, Timothy Bence, Briane Swimme, Bruce Sanguin, Ken Wilber, Teilhard de Chardin, The Universal Eileen, Barbara Marx Hubbard. What were all these voices saying in the life of Universal Man?

It just doesn’t make any logical sense to kill the poet. Because if we burn one story, or stifle one religion then we may as well go ahead and burn ALL the books and prohibit any story telling at all. And I’m sure, dear reader, that you can see how silly that is, because it strongly disagrees with our very biology, with our very evolution. We are story tellers. We are a species who is closely linked with our symbolic stories. Even our sacred practice of science is still just creating a story, a story which is fabricated out of the perception of a universe that is absolutely made of matter, or non-matter or dark matter! (I’m not a physicicist! Nor can I even spell right now! Please, someone take over here as I reveal the fragile nature of my own intellect). 

The point is simply this: things are not nearly as solid as we thought they were.

The only rational move is to allow this beautiful, rational mind to embrace this big, beautiful heart. Can we agree on that today? Can we allow this to happen. Can we allow these big beautiful mind of ours to come into communion with these soft, fleshy, vulnerable, wounded, traumatized hearts of ours? Can we agree to stop hurting ourselves in these ways?

It is this reality that may become evident as we take the medicine into our body. We may begin to see that things are not nearly as solid as we thought they were. We may begin to see the spiraling state of our being. We may begin to see that everything is turning over and over on itself, like the blueberry batter in which my mother gave me my first lesson in spiral dynamics. I was a child. This was before I was ready to understand it on a more intellectual level. But I saw it in the batter, in just the way that a child can and should see it. It was as true then as it is now. The batter showed me all I would need to know really, as far as acceptance goes, was I watch thos blueberries being folded in to the mix. But, I left the batter behind. Just as Peter would not remember the loving embrace of his lover until after he’d betrayed him three times. These are the things that must pass from our understanding so that we can live out the death that the understanding was bringing us to in the first place. We spiral upward (or downward) because that is what Life is doing in us. This is the story of how we make peace with the very nature of our being. This is how our story continues. Very gently. Very compassionately. There is nothing to believe. There is nothing we have to protect. Here we are, just allowing all the stories to come and go. We’re not taking any of this too seriously because now we’ve seen things from a deeper perspective. We’re just yawning. We’re just waking up to a new day as the sun is rising in the East. We’ve got a little smile on our face as we realize the nightmare that we thought was our life was really just a scary dream from which we are awakening.

And this is the moment, dear reader, where we may truly acknowledge that the Universal sun that is rising out there as we speak. This is the moment where we may choose to acknowledge that rising sun by stepping out on that Universal East-facing front porch. Yes, dear reader, this is the moment in which we see the orange of the sun that has yet to peak its face over the horizon, and the blues which are above those oranges. And there She is. The great tree. The One with her roots deep in the soil. The One whose branches are reaching high into the sky. This One who went into the soil so many years ago. The seed that went into the soil, died, and was reborn into this form that we now call “tree”. This deep and majestic one who speaks to us still of the great mysteries of life. And it is in this moment that we might just pull down our pants and relieve ourselves of that which is pushing on our bladder. Yes, dear reader, in this moment we just take a piss off the front porch while we witnessing this great beauty—this sacred act of urination which holds us to the spot so briefly. 

Yes, dear reader, we are connecting with the earth again. We are regaining our communion with the soil. We are returning home to the Great Mother in this way. Please, dear reader, let her help. She is ready to absorb your trauma into her great bosom. She is ready to take you in now. 

Selah

So, as we transcend the literal, as we transcend the rational, as we allow ourselves to become trans-rational beings, what we once believed to be blasphemy (blasphemy of materialism, blasphemy of religion, they have become the same now). We now see all of this process as the sacred Being of nature. This is the view we get as we are spiraling up through the great consciousness of the Great Mother. 

Dear reader, we thought that we would die if we left our old form behind, and that is true in as far as it is true. It is true that there is a death in that leaving. A death that you will certainly experience and feel in your bein. For those of us who suffer like this, this death is the very thing that we literally believe will kill us. And sometimes, I suppose it really does kill us. 
Yes, dear reader, we have all seen this happen

But, who is doing the killing there? Fear? Confusion? Hopelessness? Yes, dear reader, this is how so many of our kind have gone before us, in great hopelessness and defeat. These ones who were just out of reach. These ones who could not be held. These ones who were unable to allow the depth of holding that they needed. Those of us who were not supported in the ways that we needed. Those of us who could not ask for help. Those of us who did not know how to ask for help as they were trapped in the very state of destructive consciousness that would kill them.

You may remember these fallen ones now. Remember them that we may live now. Let there life become a testament to the rebirth that we are now experiencing.

Yes, dear reader, the death that we experience as we transcend is required. But, if we can make peace with that death in ourselves. When we begin this process of, ever so gently, inviting it in, there is a resurrection (the upward spiral) that comes afterward. This is the clear teaching of the great Tree of Life. Do not despair, this process continues in us now. There she is! The Great Mother unwinding herself in the One consciousness that is even deeper than we can humanly imagine. For, there is more to come, Dear Ones. There is more depth to be experiences. Let your curiosity be peaked. There is more to come.

Nothing has to be literal anymore. It’s all just a solid as we want it to be. The stories can come from anywhere now, any source. In fact, the stories that are not “true” are sometimes the best place to find that Being rising up from within us. And I think that this is was what Joseph Campbell was getting at when he was here.

Our brother, Joseph, helped us to see the Universal Thread that was woven into every hero’s journey in every culture. And Carl Jung too, that’s what he was getting at when he was showing us our archetypes. The Christ story is just one way that we can make peace with this Universal Being that is the I Am-ness which we all carry. We are all just trying to make peace with that which is embedded in our DNA, the great spiraling DNA as expressed through the dreams we dream and the stories that those dreams inspire. 

These universal dreams and stories are not to be believed. No. In fact, believing them does them an injustice. We are not asked to make a pledge of allegiance to any one of these stories, no dear reader, that would be so violent. That would require us to go to war again. For with each pledge of allegiance that we make, we also create another conflict in ourselves. And thes conflicts within ourselves lead to conflicts being made manifest in the world.

No, dear reader. There is no pledge of allegiance required. That is not what is happening here.

Dear reader, you are being given permission. And this is not a permission that comes from without. No, dear reader, this permission is coming from deep within your own being. Be still now. Can you feel Her arising? She is the one who gives you permission. She is the gentle one, the one who asks the questions that fill us with compassion for ourselves.

We give ourselves this permission to be intimately engaged with these stories.  We allow Presence to arise from deep within. These stories are not to be believed, they are to be experienced just as we experience a mountain in passing on our journey. These storeies were meant to be left behind. These stories were made to lead us to our deaths and to lead us to our new lives. 

The stories come and go. And we allow them to do just that, as scary as it is. If we are trying to make a religion out of any one particular story, we are nailing ourselves down, we are trying to make this a static experience, we ae trying to take a journey but refusing to let the mountain fade in our rear view mirror. Can you see this, dear reader. Can you see your freedom now? Can you see your own freedom as you allow yourself to leave those pledges of allegiance behind. Can you feel the trauma that these oaths have caused in you? Can you see the damage that these alliances to stories have caused among our people? Can you sense the young men who are dying now, over there? Do you see now how we are sending our young ones off to die lonely deaths as we perpetuate the violence?

Many Americans are able to see the destructive nature of fundamentalism when housed in a religion that is foreign to them. But we are so often unable to see the destructive nature or our own fundamentalism. Not that this is just an American problem, by any means. And it’s not a religious problem either. And maybe I shouldn’t be calling it a problem at all, dear reader. Maybe I just started speaking with a little bit more of that impatient masculine tone thatn this situation truly deserves. Maybe, dear reader, we can allow her to rise up in our midst again. Selah. Maybe it would be more helpful for us to just see it for exactly what it is, part of the evolution of consciousness. Nothing has gone terribly wrong. We are just waking up. We are yawning. Stretching our arms. Letting the sun rise on another day.

Dear reader, whenever we are absolutely certain that our story is the only story, and especially when we feel somehow obligated to defend our own story, then we are participating in fundamentalism. Whenever we are excluding others, or waiting for everyone else to join our club, then we are participating in fundamentalism. Anything less than all-inclusiveness is fundamentalism. Anything less than the wide open arms of an evolving Universal Being is fundamentalism. This is the binary. The fork in the road. The good. The evil. The limited consciousness that is still causing us to suffer so deeply.

And it was through this very intimate knowledge of his Ophidian Queen in his heart that the middle-aged man found himself making love to his Lord in the shower. This was where the middle-aged-man found himself to be. This is how it came to pass that the man found his fluidity in that one peak moment in the shower. This is how it came to pass that he continued his participation in the upward (or downward) spiral of evolving consciousness, folded over and over again into the blueberry batter that his mother had so lovingly shown him when he  was a child.

In the shower that morning, the middle-aged man integrated every form that appeared to him. He was no longer shackled by death and sin, he was experiencing the resurrection. All the forms of love-making he had once participated in or had merely heard of came to him in all their fullness. These forms were integrated in his being. This is how the self-love experience arose within him in the shower that morning. The Universe was evolving in this middle-aged-man. He was engage in a loving experience with the great What-Is. He made love to the great I AM that day in the Universal Shower.

Praise all that was and ever shall be. Praise the ALL from the rootedness of this present moment. Praise be to the ALL. May we continue in at least fifty different shades of Pray.


Day 8
3:22 PM

The man went forth into his day giving thanks and praises to his Lord and Savior. The joy of his salvation was restored unto him. He sang in the field where his great treasure was buried. He played skillfully on his stringed instrument. He sang praises to all creation in the field which held his treasure. This was what he’d sold everything for so many many years ago. 

He vamped the same two chords, singing and praying for his own salvation and also the salvation of the world. He prayed for the Great Turning. He wept in recognition of the fallen condition of his world. He prayed for all of his people. He prayed for all those people who were in his small sphere of influence. He prayed for all those people who were not. He prayed while strumming his guitar. He sang for a world that was in trouble. He prayed for the turning of his people toward the center. He proclaimed his own return, the return of the prodigal son. He sang to the blue sky. He sang to the evergreens. He sang to the sun. He sang to the jet passing overhead and prayed for everyone on board. He knew that all his prayers mattered. He was fully present. He was experiencing the fullness of life. The joy of his salvation was returned to him.

The man continued in his preparation for his journey. He folded laundry, treating every shirt with love and respect, speaking to each one and reminding them of the memories that they shared with him. 

There was the sea turtle shirt, which had come from the big island of Hawaii, where his family had gone on their last family vacation. With the shirt came a vision of two turtles, on male, the other female. They rested in large tidal pool. The male was still. The female swam to and fro in the pool. Young girls jumped in after her. The surface of the water was rippled in patterns by the Pacific breeze blowing in from the blue.

There was also his Blackwater Draw Boys three-quarter-sleeve, which was born from the love that he made with those boys from that band years ago, those deep and powerful boys of the Eastern High Plains who made such a thunderous sound for a brief moment.  Praise the boys of the Blackwater Draw.

And then there was this comfy two-tone blue fleece that he’d had for over twenty years now. She just kept asking to be worn. The middle-aged-man realized his love for this shirt, a form that would eventually pass.

He packed these shirts in his suitcase in preparation for his journey. He packed his socks. He packed his underwear. And he packed his favorite pair of pants that he had worn the morning after he’d taken that “lethal dose” of mushrooms, lethal in the sense that he was afraid that he would not come back. He’d had heard of people not coming back. He’d heard that sometimes it took a while to come back. Hospitalization possibly? That’s what he had been most afraid of that Christmas morning--A psychosis like that.  He didn’t know for sure if all that was true. But, the fear was real that he’d felt. It was the dose that felt lethal, that in the very least caused him to feel as if he were going to die.

The man tidied the great living room. He put his things away. He cleared the house for Dan’s guests who were scheduled to arrive tomorrow. The man made his bed. He folded all the blankets. He arranged the altar that he had constructed in his closet months ago, where he had burned sage and rang his singing bell and meditated. He then prepared a grilled cheese sandwich and some tomato soup for a late afternoon meal. 

As he prepared the meal he asked God, “How will I live? Is there a plan? What will I do when my car insurance is due? What will I do when the child support is due? What is the plan? Is there any way that we can plan together? Are you into that kind of thing.”

All the man’s energy was going into keeping himself in a right mind. He was using all the tools that he had acquired over the years in his spiritual readings, from his psychotherapy sessions, his musical skills, his writing skills, His yoga. He was experiencing some kind of flow today. There was much effortlessness in the way he moved from task to task. But then he got tired. 

He wondered if he was living at the speed of life. He knew that the writing he was doing was helping him. As he wrote about his experiences he was encouraged. Writing was one way that the man was renewing his mind. He was witness to the miracles that were taking place in his own heart as these stories were opening up to him. As he allowed the wisdom to come forth, he saw his story in a different light. He felt that he was performing the miracle that Stephen Schettini had asked of him several years ago. Stephen Schettini, the non-theist monk who asked the middle-aged man for a miracle. “Ha,” he thought out loud, “A non-theist monk asking for a miracle. Was that not a little miracle in itself?”

There are no little miracles, he reminded himself. All miracles are equal, and all flowing from the One Source of Love. All Praise the great mother of the Course! She whose name was not known until after her passing from this world, Helen Schucman. Praise be to the great Mother of wisdom.

The middle-aged man’s experience at cabin weeks ago opened his heart toward his father. Their texts were recorded like this:

Son: I’d like to see you soon. I don’t want anything from you. I just want to stand in your presence

You see, dear reader, the middle-aged-man hurt his father very deeply when he’d asked him for some money. This all happened about a year ago. The man was in crisis again after giving himself away to his Bermudian lover. The man’s ego was out of control, he’d allowed thoughts of suicide back in. The man wanted help from his father. It was with a true sense of desire to be connected with his father that the son reached out. 

But, the son ended up asking his father for some money. When the son asked for money it triggered deep anger and resentment in the father. The father melted down in a booth at a mexican restaurant, with a shaking voice and tears in his eyes, the son, once again blamed his son for his misery. “You are the reason that I am a miserable man. Do you know how long I’ve wanted to put a bullet in your head? And your mother too, a bullet in her head, too.” The son was not aware that the father had wanted to put a bullet in the heads of himself or the mother.

The son, who had called the father for help, breathed deeply as his father chastised him for quite some time. The father would not let his son talk. The son only tried to speak in return once. He was shooshed. “No, you listen to me right now,” said the father. And so the son remember his Vipassana practice and sat there in the booth for a very long time.

When the father was finished, his son said, “Father, I am sorry for all the pain that I have caused you.”

In the wake of that event, the father communicated very clearly that he did not want his son to ask for anything from him again. This is why the son clearly stated, “I don’t want anything from you” in the text that he’d sent to his father.

“Mother?” the middle-aged-man opened himself up to the inner Wisdom, “Mother, will you be with me as I make this journey toward my father? Strengthen me. Please hold me as we watch this flower together. Please give me a heart for my father.

“Yes Son, we will pray for your father. He suffers very deeply. He is one of the hurting ones.”

“Yes, Mother. Please pray for my father. Fill my heart with love for this man who did the best that he could with what he was given. I give you thanks for my father.

And then the father responded to the son’s text:

Father: sounds good! Next time you are in OKC you are welcome to stop by…appreciate a heads up if you do…Probably be more sitting in my presence in liew of standing up…up to you I guess…haha

Days later, after some further exchanges of addresses and information the conversation went like this:

Son: How far in advance do you need to know I’m coming? Respectfully

Father: are you in my driveway?

Son: No. LOL. Really. LOL. Oh man. That’s funny. I love it.
Are you in my driveway? Oh my gosh…that is the punchline to my morning! It just keeps delivering. (for some reason this really tickled the son. His heart was set on his father again..
Are you in my driveway! Oh my goodness. Real mileage. Thank you, father.

Dad: you can come by anytime…any day…as often as you like…no notice necessary…I’m thinking of replacing the couch in the back bedroom with a bed so you’ll be comfortable…Come by anytime. No notice required…Pop ins welcome

Son: Oh man. That’s quite an invitation. I gratefully accept your invitation. I gratefully receive all the life that you have freely shared with me. Thank you, father.

Father: how quick do I need to switch out the couch for a bed?

Son: There’s no need to rush out and change the furniture just yet. But, I accept the invitation nonetheless, in its fullness. I accept the heart of the invitation with an open heart of gratitude…I am truly grateful for all the gifts that you have freely shared with me….I am grateful to you for the space that you held, for the sufferings that you endured to keep a roof over our heads…Today I am gifted with a heart of gratitude. I acknowledge the life that you shared with all of us…4….That number was special to me. It was the number of “us.” It was the count…Then my number became 5…5 was the count…and I still hold that count in my heart…with as much grace and forgiveness for myself as I can channel today. There has been a lot of suffering. A lot of anger. A lot to bear. I have come to know this suffering intimately in myself. I am making peace with this suffering in myself today…Holding the joy in one hand. Holding the the suffering in the other…living with them both…coexisting with all of that. Just for today…just for this moment…just in this breath…I do not know what the next moment brings…

The son was really blowing up his dad’s phone! He continued:

Son: I’ve spent a lot of time in prayer and meditation over the last year…December 27th 2018 I went to the psychiatric unit. Got out on new years day. Then I went to a(nother) treatment center. January 8th through february 5th…These dates are in my memory…I’ve been making peace with the fabric that holds us together…Making peace with the Father. The Mother. And somehow making peace within myself. I baptized mysef in a bathtub a few weeks ago. Then I went to a church and got baptized with witnesses. Then I baptized myself just last weekend in a cold mountain stream, in my underwear, like a wildman, running for the cover of trees as a car passed by on the highway just fifty feet away. 40 degrees outside. Then I climbed a mountain in dialogue with the Father. “Father?” “Yes, Son”…and in doing so I have made a deeper peace with you

Father: I am grateful at this moment that you are talking to me…It strengthens me…I feel so lost when we don’t text..Kathy and I hate each other…truly…she told me God gives her strength every day to put up with me…she is a gift from God…I jumped into this marriage 25 years ago cause I was insecure and my feelings were hurt by dismissal from my life of 4…This marriage was a mistake…I just have to wake up and move on…I don’t understand my hesitation…maybe this year…You are important to me. I have gathered my sacred matter. The car is fueled. The duct tape is in the car. My instruments are packed. I AM. I AM in the first person. The great What-IS. 

Off to meet the Universal Mother.
Off to meet the Universal Father.
Off to travel with the Universal Sons and Daughters.

May God bless our travels
May God fill our sight and hearing.
May God guide our steps and light our way.
May God rise up from within us and all who we meet.
May the Christ be glorified.
May the Christ be known in us.
Please take from us our lives when we have not the strength to give it away to you, Oh God.

And, Oh yes, Dave and Eileen. If everything goes as planned I will see you in Bentonville.

Day 10 
12: 39AM

The middle aged man, the son, the father sat, facing North once again. Samuel Barber’s Adagio for strings played in his ears, the sacred sound of his resurrection. He sat in his mother’s home, which in the previous hours was filled with the joyful sounds of his growing and grown children, those children who had arrived at his house early that morning bearing gifts, Christmas gifts which they had brought to their father, the man who had walked away from their mutual home just twenty months ago, the man who now lived at the Adobe style house with the Saltillo tile, the man who now sat facing North on his mother’s couch which had been turned into his bed for tonight.

The adagio ended. He sat. He breathed in the silence. As he read through the first chapters of his draft he was overcome by two things:

1.	 the gravity of the Presence that he was discovering in this process. He found himself in communion with the All.

2.	 The immensity of work that the draft would still require in order to prepare it suitably for you, dear reader. 


The man arose from his seat on the couch and moved about his mother’s home. His Children were sleeping in the two spare bedrooms. He got the couch in main living room. 

Do you see the man walking about his mother’s home. Yes, he is seeking out a quiet place for communion. It has been a big day of travel and conversation. He seeks a quiet place. 

The man repositioned the floor mat in the bathroom. He lowered himself to his knees. He kneeled deeply. His body folded, his head partially on the cold floor tile. He allowed the I AM to be, or rather, he witnessed the I AM, the Being-ness of this consciousness. 

The man was grateful for this form which, in the past, he had called the water-droplet position, sometimes imagining himself a drop of dew resting on a blade of grass. His mind, of course, wandered, but the pose reminded him of his intention. 

Yes, der reader, as he awoke from the wanderings of his mind, the pose reminded him of the possibility of simple-being. The man kneeled on the bathroom floor in his mother’s home that Friday evening. He kneeled in all gratefulness for what he had experienced during the course of the day. He was in communion with the great What-IS.

The gifts that his children brought to him that morning made him very glad. His children stacked the gifts on the kitchen table in the Adobe house on avenue Q. He opened the smallest gift first, as it was at the top of the pyramid. It was small and light. 

“It’s a zippo lighter,” he said jokingly. 

The small package was actually much lighter than a zippo lighter would have been. The father tore the paper and opened the tiny box. Inside was a small piece of unusually shaped and stained-wood. The small piece of wood had a hole in the middle big enough for his middle finger. 

This was a minimalist gift, given to him by his son, its purpose--to hold the pages of a book apart as the reader held the book in a single hand. The father, knowing the difficulty of reading in bed while trying to hold open both sides of a book with two hands understood the importance of the gift. And as he sat on his mother’s couch now, turning it over and over in his hands, he was grateful for the gift from his son. He was grateful for its simplicity. He was grateful for its function. He was grateful that his son knew that he loved to read. He accepted the unusual gift. He loved his son.

The next gift was housed in a significantly larger box, it was from his youngest daughter. “Oh, I know exactly what this is,” the father said. “But I’m not going to say,” another attempt at a joke. He loved to hear his children laugh. 

As the lid came off the box, he saw a treasure trove. There was a wood patterned capo, just what he had asked for when his youngest had inquired about what he wanted for Christmas. There was a guitar strap decorated with golden suns. And, the gift that most impressed the father, held in it’s own cloth bag, a gorgeous leather bound journal, which looked like it was deserving to carry a sacred message. 

Facing his youngest daughter, the father held the book out in front of him in both hands and bowed. “I present to you the new gospel,” he said in a lofty tone. This time his children actually laughed and the father was well pleased. His youngest daughter had often impressed him with her generosity. He was powerfully moved by her ability to find gifts that mattered to him. He was grateful for her thoughtfulness. He was grateful for her.

The next present, the largest box, had functioned as the base of the Christmas pyramid. The father casually reached out to pick it up but it was very heavy. He repositioned himself so that he could lift the dense package from the table. He tore the paper from the cardboard box which was neatly taped shut. 

He remembered that his pocket knife was clipped on to his right pocket. He grabbed the pocket knife and flipped it open, as he had learned to do as a young police officer. He cut the tape and opened the box. Inside was something that he had mentioned to his oldest daughter that he wanted while they made the cheesecake to accompany their Christmas taco-Tuesday meal. It was a weighted blanket! The daughter had already, in fact, ordered it for him as they were preparing the meal together. The eldest daughter and her husband were well pleased with themselves, as they should have been. 

Once again, in an attempt to be the cause of his children’s laughter, the father removed the weighted blanket from its packaging and proceeded to lie down in a supine position right there on the hard kitchen tile. He pulled the blanket up o his chin, and his children did laugh with their father, and the father was very glad indeed.

The father had not expected his children’s gifts to bring him such joy. And so it came to pass that the family, having completed the exchange of their Christmas gifts piled in their two little blue cars and headed for the home of the children’s grandmother.

The first several hours of the journey were covered by dense fog and light rain. The oldest daughter rode with the father with her father. The Prince of Egypt soundtrack played as they drove (It was one of the current soundtracks of his eldest daughter’s life). And so it was that the father and his oldest daughter drove East, that day toward Oklahoma City on the same roads that the father had hitchhiked  two weeks before, listening to music that contained the story of Moses. And the father heard his own story in these songs. And the father laughed and wept for joy as he saw his story in this Universal story. And he heard the depth, and darkness, and mystery in those melodies that day.

The father was able to share the depths of his heart with his daughter that day, and his daughter had a heart to hear her father, and the father was very glad. And the daughter, although she was generally a very quiet person, also shared her heart with her father. And the father felt more known by his daughter. And the father also felt that he knew his oldest daughter a little more.

The daughter shared her grief at her father’s timing in the leaving of his family, for the father had dissolved his own marriage just one month prior to her own marriage. The father asked for forgiveness that he had hurt her in this way. He asked her how he could make it right. The daughter said that she was no longer angry, although she had been very angry with the him at the time.

The man and his children stopped to eat at Subway, all four of them, and they laughed at each other’s ridiculous jokes. It was a hearty laughter that resounded throughout the Subway in the gas station somewhere along the foggy highway which lead them East toward the children’s grandmother and their belated Christmas celebration.
Editor’s note: On the editor’s timeline it was 12:05AM on January 15th , nearly three weeks after the trip that the father made with his children to Oklahoma city in the fog. The medicine ceremony ended (“let’s see,” said the editor “when did it end?” the editor tried to piece it together….the timeline was foggy, like the car ride of nearly three weeks ago, he couldn’t see  very far in his rearview mirror)…there! He had it now…the medicine ceremony ended just about thirty two hours ago on January 13th at 4pm (the details, he realized were probably not too important to his reader, but he was now in survival mode, and so he clung to the details that he found in the timeline. The details seemed to have some kind of grounding affect in his being). 

The editor had not edited or worked on his book for thirty eight hours, the longest that he’d been away from this work since he wrote that fateful email to Dave and Eileen (Eileen and Dave). The letter was written on December 18th, exactly four weeks ago today. 

After the end of the medicine ceremony (or the initiation ceremony as he was now calling it) he had taken a day off. He took care of practical matters. He visited the human resources department at the hospital where he was still employed but on medical leave. He turned in paper work in order to get all the money that he could from his retirement account, possibly as much as $2600. He visited the University to see about the job that he’d been offered there to teach as a part time adjunct professor, meeting with the chair of the department and Richard Schwartz, the man (his friend) he’d be assisting.

The last four days of the ceremony had involved marijuana, the substance that the middle-aged-man had referred to in his AA past as his “drug of choice.” He obtained some marijuana and a pipe from Joann. Then his inner voices began to encourage him to smoke (that inner voice being represented by the Great Mother and the beloved Muse and finally an inner spirit-shaman who encouraged him to return to the pipe much more than he was comfortable with in the final ceremony.

The marijuana helped the editor to get in touch with those hurting parts of himself. The initial smoke had much to do with him facing many of his issues that came up as a result of smoking. His twelve-step-program required abstinence. A substance was being re-introduced that he had become willing to believe was part of his problem. However, even the AA literature stated that the substance is not the problem, that is was selfishness and self-centeredness that was the problem. Therefore it seemed to the man who had attended so many AA meetings, that if the ego problem was taken care of, then the substance should not be a problem. However, these were only memories to the editor as he began to hit the pipe again. He reached out to find his old friend marijuana with a very visceral sensation that told him that this is what he needed.

And this is how the editor came to wrap up the medicine ceremony after making peace with his “drug of choice” for four days. You can see, dear reader, the risks that the editor was taking. 

There he sits, the editor. He sits in the great room with the vaulted ceiling. He is breathing. He is writing, adding new material to the text as he is clinging once again to this project. He went to bed last night with his hurting children screaming in fear. There he sits, the editor, parenting the hurting inner children that he’d become so familiar with in the last four days of the ceremony. There he sits, the editor and his “escapist” subpersonality, the one who would have him continue smoking the rest of the marijuana that he has in the closet despite the fact that the ceremony is over. But the nurturing adult is there too. The nurturing adult is caring for the escapist. He is caring for the little trembling child that appeared at the very end of the ceremony, making it known that he was still in need of comforting and help.

Yes, dear reader, there he sits wearing the snake ring that he had lost. The snake ring was returned to him by Dan as he’d slept. Dan entered. The alarm, which the middle-aged-man rarely set woke him from his sleep.

“Hey Dan,” the editor called from his bed.

“Hey bud, I found this ring in the truck. I think it’s yours. It’s your snake ring.”

Although his wounded inner-children were crying, the man acknowledged this as a gesture from the Universe. He slipped the snake ring on. He gave thanks. He went back to sleep

Another bit of information that the editor returns here to note (as he sits in the West Forest in the early hours of the morning with his laptop on his weighted blanket) is that on this day the ACIM lesson was this: “I cannot know the meaning of ________.” This is significant in the healing process because as the writer was writing the letters that morning he was professing that he did not know the meaning of these emails he was sending. He was in the process of surrendering the project. He was in the process of allowing the project to be what it needed to be for his healing. In the spirit of Bill’s Wild Mind, the editor was now in the process of congratulating his wounded inner-children, those subpersonalities, on the great job that they had done. The medicines were clearing the mans body. He was re-entering the world. There had been moments of bliss as he re-entered. Then the pain that he was so familiar had returned. He spent the morning in the release of the writing project. He sent several emails out, attaching the portion of this book that had been edited up to that point. Good morning, Bruce

This initiation ceremony that I have been on came to a close yesterday. I share this with you for at least two reasons.

1. I acknowledge that in my past I have lacked the wisdom and depth-of-healing necessary to treat the medicine as ceremonial. Therefore, as I announce the end of this ceremony with another human being who has a context for this experience, I am making myself accountable in what I hope to be the beginnings of a new community that is arising in my consciousness.

2. As this initiation ceremony has come to an end (with great reverential joy and celebration I may add!) I find myself in awe of what I have seen. I acknowledge the sacred ceremony that I was given through the Wisdom Spirit that rose from within my being. However, as I acknowledge the wisdom that comes from within, I also acknowledge the need for wisdom to flow into this individual being from outside sources. And it is in this spirit that I feel urged to reach out in an effort to connect with a teacher/teachers who can help me to develop this new understanding which I have recently come in to.

Today, I am at peace. I reached out to receive healing from the One Source and our loving Source, She responded in a way that I could feel in my body. I felt myself receiving Her help. The help that I have needed for all my life--the help that no human, no group of humans, and no form could offer to me (although I acknowledge that this help was not separate from all the help that I have received from those  "others" and those "forms" that I have left behind). I have finally (again) discovered my own help rising up from deep within my being.

Today, as I acknowledge the strength and health found in my own inner depths I am also reaching out to those "others" who may be able to help me to develop this further. As the ceremony ended the "scared (sacred) child" in me still trembled with fear. I know there is more help that he needs. How many times, Bruce, have I had a significant spiritual experience and hoped to G_d that the suffering was over? I humbly acknowledge the depth and power of this initiation ceremony that I have completed, as I acknowledge that this healing must continue for me.

I read a book by a guy named Paul Francis, Therapeutic Shamanism. I liked how he did not call himself a Shaman. He said that he had a Therapeutic Shamanistic practice. This was the way he practiced these ancient experiences while acknowledging that the appropriation of the title of Shaman was destructive.

It is within that context established for me by Paul Francis I find myself today. I have had this profound experience in which these medicines (mushrooms, LSD, and marijuana) have opened my heart and mind to a deeper experience of reality. These medicines, in conjunction with the living Spirit that I encountered in nature allowed me to encounter a healing Being. I was embraced by this Healing. I was given visions. I was held like I have never been able to hold myself, yet this holding came very much from within myself. As I acknowledge the gift of my own power, I would very much like to encounter those "others" out there who have more wisdom, who have history and deeper understanding of these connections that I have so recently made. 

I want to be taught. I want to have more teachers. I need to know this for myself. I need to be in community where there is understanding for this. My heart longs to find brothers and sisters who know this experience.

I have left everything. That's not true. Let me try again.

I am making peace with this process of awakening. I am giving myself permission to live at the speed of life.

What is it that you said in your book? "I bow. I become hospitable? I allow the Greater Power to live life through me?" Did I miss one, Bruce? was there four? I tried to go back and find that place in your book, couldn't find it. 

Is the fourth one, living at the speed of life? 

This email is a prayer, Bruce. No, I'm not praying to you. Well, maybe that is what I am doing. What I mean is, I am reaching out to you as you are this "other" human being who embodies the Universal Christ. And your voice was a powerful companion on this initiation ceremony. This is a prayer that needs to be witnessed by another human being who has a context for what I'm saying, and your voice provided a powerful context in which my ceremony took place. Is there any context for this, Bruce?

Is there a place in this world for a middle-aged-man who has dropped his fishing-nets to follow the Universal Christ? The response that he received from Bruce caused the editor’s wounded inner child to hurt. It was rejection that came up. Yes, it was rejection that he was dealing with. Bruce’s response caused the editor to have to begin to struggle. Here is what Bruce said:

Hi Travis, 

Thanks for your email. At this stage of my life, I'm a psychotherapist. I understand the need for the kind of community that you desire. I'm not set up to provide that.  I can be your therapist, and make a place for you in my heart, yes. My commitments at this time don't allow me the time to read manuscripts, although I did read through your opening and your email to your publisher friends, along with her compassionate and encouraging response.

 I fall into that "thru-line" preference as a writer and reader. The stream of consciousness feel often ends up, in my experience, privileging self-consciousness over Self-awareness (if I'm understanding your distinction). I will say, that as an unexpected author, I was amazed at how much more work it is to edit than to write. Editing brings clarity to what it is exactly I'm trying to communicate, and it can't be everything. The depth and quality of your experience in the medicine are unquestionable. As is your capacity to write. At some point, although it's a memoir-style, it has to be for the reader, or the reader has to be able to locate him/herself, not necessarily in your exact experience, but in your reflection on the experience that hopefully identifies something universal about Reality, and which the reader can say, "Yeah, he really gets it. He really gets me".  That's why people read I suspect.  

I hear that on your medicine journeys I've shown up as a guide or a voice? Is that accurate? I'm no Chief, however.  I'm just Bruce, offering to contain people as they suffer what's theirs to suffer and make deeper contact with the life they've been given. If that's something you're interested in, I'm happy to work with you. 

Love, 

Bruce
